What stays open is promptness, not the duty: a run whose retries keep failing has no verdict until 05.3.2-T3.1 forces one at the deadline. | Connect-ci page spec does not assert the health timer cap (09.2.1-T7, S77, #168, da30c94) | The credential-boundary timer is capped at `MAX_HEALTH_TIMEOUT_MS = 2 ** 31 - 1` (`apps/web/src/app/features/onboarding/steps/connect-ci.page.ts:43`), because a longer `setTimeout` delay fires at once. The old spec asserted the capped delay through a `setTimeout` spy; that spy leaked fake timers into `core/telemetry/telemetry.spec.ts` in the full vitest run, so the specs now count timers with `vi.getTimerCount()` and the cap value itself is asserted nowhere (fake timers do not emulate the overflow). A real-timer or extracted-helper test is the fix. | Federated login is single-node safe only (02.3.1-T1.1 hand-over, S70) | The one-time `state` replay cache (`Login/LoginReplayCache.cs`) is in-process and the login state cookie is protected by ASP.NET Data Protection, whose key ring must be the same on every node and persisted (in a container without a writable key directory the default ring is in memory and pending logins do not survive a restart); the per-IP login throttle keys on `RemoteIpAddress`, so behind a load balancer it also needs `UseForwardedHeaders` with a known-proxy list, and it shares the CI exchange limiter's 50 000-key pool (login needs its own limiter instance or per-prefix cap). Prod runs at least 2 `Evidgate.Api` replicas (plan 10 FR-10-1). Needed before a second replica serves `/v1/auth/oidc/*`; #164 adds that the prod load balancer already makes every anonymous caller share one `login-cb` bucket and puts the balancer's address in `session.ip` until forwarded headers land. S81 adds the link path to the same dependency: the linking routes replay-protect through the SHARED callback's one-time `state` (`src/Modules/Evidgate.Modules.Identity/Endpoints/LoginEndpoints.cs:372`, `replay.TryConsume(provider + ":" + state)`), and the staged `__Host-evidgate_link` / `__Host-evidgate_invite` cookies are Data Protection values under their own purposes, so a node without the shared key ring cannot read another node's staging. S81 adds four limiter prefixes to that shared pool under the same `RemoteIpAddress` key, each behind its own prefix so one route cannot spend another's budget: `login-invite:` (`InviteTokenEndpoints`) and `login-link-start:` / `login-link-pending:` (both pending routes) / `login-link-confirm:` (`LinkEndpoints`). | Session mutation CSRF scope: an `Authorization` header takes a cookie-authenticated mutation out of CSRF scope (02.3.2-T2 security review F3, S102, #228) | severity low / latent. `SessionXsrfPolicy.IsInScope` exempts any request that carries `Authorization` (`src/Modules/Evidgate.Modules.Identity/Sessions/Http/SessionXsrfPolicy.cs:54`, inside `IsInScope` `:49-57`), so a browser able to attach a bearer header would take a cookie-authenticated mutation out of the antiforgery pair. Latent, not exploitable today: no CORS policy is configured (`AddCors`/`UseCors` appear nowhere in `src/`), so a browser cannot attach the header cross-site, and a bearer request is authenticated by the bearer, not by the session cookie. Fix shape: check the pair whenever the `__Host-evidgate_session` cookie is present, regardless of the header — needs a T1 policy test plus a review of the CI-token routes that legitimately carry a bearer | receiver: 11.7.1-T2 as a HAND-OVER only — **no existing sub-task of 11.7.1-T2 covers this** (T2.1 is CodeQL/Roslyn/ESLint SAST and T2.2 a weekly passive ZAP baseline, which cannot confirm a header-dependent bypass); the hand-over adds a sub-item "CSRF scope review of cookie+`Authorization` requests and a policy test" (fix shape above) to T2's scope, recorded in `plans/features/11-security-compliance/11.7.1-proved-against-isolation-not-asserted-acceptance.md:152`. No `02.3.2-T3` exists in `plan_graph` (the 02.3.2 story is T1 and T2 only); hand-over Notes: `plans/features/02-identity-tenancy/02.3.2-sessions-and-revocation.md:167` |
| Database fault on session routes surfaces as 500 not 503 (02.3.2-T2 security review F6, S102, #228) | severity low. A database fault during any of the four routes falls to the catch-all of `EnvelopeExceptionMiddleware` (`src/Evidgate.Api/Middleware/EnvelopeExceptionMiddleware.cs:75`), so the client gets `500 internal_error` with a `request_id` instead of a retryable `503` with `Retry-After`; the body is the fixed `internal_error` envelope (`src/BuildingBlocks/SharedKernel/Api/ErrorEnvelope.cs:183`), so nothing internal leaks. Fix shape: map the transient `NpgsqlException` classes to 503 `service_unavailable` with `Retry-After` ahead of the catch-all, proved by a fault-injected handler test | receiver: 10.6.1-T4 as a HAND-OVER only — it does **not** cover this today (T4.2 verifies ingest-quota 429/503 with `Retry-After`); the hand-over adds a fault-injected handler test plus the transient-`NpgsqlException`→503 mapping to T4.2's scope, recorded in `plans/features/10-operations/10.6.1-proving-the-pilot-load.md:131`. | Reconcile fan-out and scan cost (05.3.2-T2.1/T2.2 review, S119; PerfRev P1 partial, P6, P2/P4; S123 follow-up) | Finalize enqueues one `orchestrator.run.reconcile` row per newly closed slot, so N shards run N serialized reconciles; each reads the run's events in one grouped statement under the run row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which blocks ingest and the other shards' finalize for the scan (PerfRev P1, SecRev F2). P1 is PARTIAL: done = no raw event copy (`as materialized` removed), the scan reads `test.started` rows only for attempts, and — since #257 — a compared attempt decides ONCE per run: the first attempt that runs with every planned shard closed (or past the deadline) freezes, appends the revision, moves the pointer and transitions, and a later trigger on the terminal run answers `NotCollectable` before any read (`Reconcile_AfterCommit_SecondTriggerNotCollectableAndNoSecondRevision`, `Reconcile_TwoConcurrentAttempts_AppendExactlyOneRevision`), so the T4.1 remainder is fewer revisions rather than fewer triggers; a `Pending` attempt still runs the aggregate and the full comparison for its log counts (S123DeltaSec N2). LEFT = the lock hold itself and N triggers per run (collapse to one task per run, natural key `(run_id)`); P6, an unlocked state peek (the `IngestEventBatch` pattern) so duplicate triggers on terminal runs do not take the lock; P2/P4, a partial index `(org_id, run_id, producer_id, execution_key, attempt_no) where type in ('test.started','test.finished')` that needs a migration (this lane held none). Plan 05.3.2 Notes (05.3.2-T2.1 + T2.2, S119) and (05.3.2-T2.3 + T2.4, S123) | receiver: 05.3.2-T4.1 (P1 remainder, P2/P4 partial index migration); 05.3.2-T3.1 (P6 unlocked state peek); hand-over Notes: `plans/features/05-runs-orchestration/05.3.2-finalize-and-reconciliation.md:205` |
| `unknown_status` definition and event counting (05.3.2-T2.2, S119, D-S119-Q1 coordinator-decided; Q2 implemented in #257) | Q1 (D-S119-Q1, coordinator-decided, owner confirmation pending; option A extended with (b)): `unknown_status` = in a closed slot a `test.finished` with NULL `normalized_status`, or a `test.started` attempt with no `test.finished` from any producer of the run. Q2 (proposed in S119, implemented in #257 and recorded below as D-S123-Q3): every stored event (late, after_cancel, stale) still counts as acknowledged — and the S119 risk is now closed rather than documented: the aggregate counts `stale = true` rows and `blocked` `test.finished` rows (`ReconcileSql.cs:88-107`), and the comparer turns a non-zero count on a closed shard into `stale_epoch` / `setup_error` (`ReconcileComparer.cs:107-127`), so a stale event that filled a sequence slot can no longer make the evidence look complete (`Reconcile_StaleEvent_CountsAgreeAndReportsStaleEpoch`). The counting rule itself stays an owner decision. Plan 05.3.2 Notes (05.3.2-T2.1 + T2.2, S119) and (05.3.2-T2.3 + T2.4, S123) | receiver: Owner (Q1 and Q2 confirmation, phase review); hand-over Notes: `plans/features/05-runs-orchestration/05.3.2-finalize-and-reconciliation.md:202`, `:203` |
| Reconcile result consumers (05.3.2-T2.1/T2.2 hand-over, S119) | Closed by 05.3.2-T2.3/T2.4 (#257, S123): `ReconcileComparison.Missing` (event reasons) is extended with the freeze reasons and the unsatisfiable marker, and a `Compared` attempt freezes, inserts one `evidence_revision`, moves `run.current_evidence_revision_id` and transitions `collecting → complete\|incomplete` in the same tenant transaction (`ReconcileFinisher.cs:105-251`), so a run whose shards close, or whose deadline has passed when a trigger arrives, no longer stays `collecting` after the task is `done`. Still open: nothing re-enqueues a reconcile at `collection_deadline_at`, so a run whose shards never close, or whose events arrive after the last finalize, is decided only if another trigger arrives — 05.3.2-T3.1 (row "Runs stuck in collecting until the deadline trigger"). | receiver: 05.3.2-T3.1 (deadline trigger); hand-over Notes: `plans/features/05-runs-orchestration/05.3.2-finalize-and-reconciliation.md:204` |

| Runs stuck in collecting until the deadline trigger (05.3.2-T2.3/T2.4 pending rule, S123) | #257's pending rule decides nothing while a planned shard is open and `now() < collection_deadline_at`, so a run whose last shard never finalizes stays `collecting` forever unless another trigger arrives: finalize enqueues one `orchestrator.run.reconcile` row per newly closed slot and nothing re-enqueues at the deadline. The same holds for AC7's finalize-before-events ordering: events that arrive after the last finalize reach no new trigger, so such a run is decided only on the data present when a trigger happens to run. Past the deadline the existing attempt already answers `incomplete` with `missing_shard` (`deadline_passed` is evaluated by the run-lock statement, `ReconcileSql.cs:40`; `Reconcile_OpenShardPastDeadline_IncompleteWithMissingShard`), so what is missing is the enqueue, not the verdict. | receiver: 05.3.2-T3.1 (deadline enforcement and retry-until-deadline); 05.3.2-T4.1 for the out-of-order trigger path; hand-over Notes: `plans/features/05-runs-orchestration/05.3.2-finalize-and-reconciliation.md:213` |
| Unknown policy artifact kind list (S123) | The policy body schema accepts any `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$` kind (`contracts/schemas/policy-revision.v1.json`), while `ArtifactKind` is a closed list `internal` to the Evidence module (`src/Modules/Evidgate.Modules.Evidence/Domain/Artifact.cs:7`); #257 turns the resulting `Validate` refusal into an unsatisfiable set — the catch re-freezes with no requirements and the finisher adds one `artifact_missing` — so the revision is `incomplete` and the run leaves `collecting` instead of dead-lettering, but the refusal drops the requirement set WHOLE: a policy mixing known and unknown kinds gets none of its kinds checked in that revision (`RequiredArtifactResolver.cs:137-161`, `ReconcileFinisher.cs:118-165`). Durable fix, not started, and it belongs to the policy side: a required kind outside Evidence's closed list is a policy defect that the evaluator reports as its own `reasons[]` entry (never as an unexplained completeness failure of the run), and the closed kind list is either exported through `Evidence.Contracts` so 05 can keep the satisfiable pairs or validated at policy write. Copying the kind list into Orchestrator stays rejected. | receiver: 07.2.1-T2 (completeness/outcome/decision computation + `reasons[]` catalogue: unknown required kind = policy defect to report; kind list exported via `Evidence.Contracts` or validated at policy write); hand-over Notes: `plans/features/07-policy-decisions/07.2.1-pure-evaluation-completeness-outcome-decision.md:101` |

| Out-of-process parse rejects after ready (S125, 06.3.6-T1.4) | With `EVIDGATE__PARSE__OUTOFPROCESS` on, an artifact is `ready` before its `artifact_parse` task finishes, so a reconcile or freeze can count it and a later `ready -> rejected` does not change an immutable frozen revision (a reconcile that runs after the reject does not count it: `EvidenceParseTaskTests`). | runs stuck in running/queued past collection_deadline_at (S152, CR-10) | The collection-deadline sweep (#330, 212f2788) scans `state = 'collecting'` only, because the finisher moves only `collecting → complete\|incomplete` and any other state answers `NotCollectable`. A run stuck in `running` or `queued` past `collection_deadline_at` (executor died before the finalize that opens collecting) still gets no verdict. Coordinator decision D-S152-Q1 (pick A); closing it needs running/queued → incomplete edges in the run state machine | closed (#348, b9b30f0c): the sweep scans queued/running/collecting and closes overdue queued/running as `incomplete` (migration 0058) |
| collection-deadline outbox key not re-armed after dead-letter (S152, CR-10) | The deadline reconcile is enqueued once per run with outbox key `orchestrator:run:{run}:reconcile:deadline`, and the sweep's probe skips a run whose key exists in any state. If that task dead-letters, the run stays `collecting` and is never re-queued. Fix: re-arm on dead-letter (attempt-suffixed key or a bounded attempt counter) with a test that forces the dead-letter | closed (#348, b9b30f0c): spent deadline attempts re-arm with `...:deadline:{n}`, bounded at 3, warning 2427 |
